    LPS-123875 Partially revert LPS-121293, specifically commit 60c8426bd04a3fb4d32b8bcfaa0f58e04ccb9e50.  This change caused a regression by expiring all article versions being published to live if one or more versions were expired.  This means if we had valid approved versions along with one or more expired versions, all versions ended up expired in live upon publication.  Further, reverting this commit does not regress LPS-120527, which is what LPS-121293 set to achieve.

commit 7e7039197ca82a63631b3ab5fc8af26ccdc7508e
Author: Julien Castelain <julien.castelain@liferay.com>
Date:   Mon Jul 26 12:46:11 2021 +0200

    LPS-136065 Adds the `showDeltasDropDown` attibute in `ClayPaginationBarTag`
    
    @liferay-echo [asked us](https://github.com/liferay/clay/issues/4175) to mark
    the `paginationBarDeltas` attribute as optional in the `ClayPaginationBarTag`
    in order to complete [LPS-123825](https://issues.liferay.com/browse/LPS-123825),
    after discusing this in https://github.com/liferay/clay/pull/4178 and
    https://github.com/liferay-frontend/liferay-portal/pull/1276, we decided
    that it would be better to add a new `showDeltasDropDown` prop in order
    to avoid breaking changes
    
    The [fix](https://github.com/liferay/clay/pull/4178) has been sent to the
    clay repository for the React component so here we're making the same change in the tag,
    we probably want to wait for the change in Clay to be in dxp before
    merging this one, although there's no real risk of breaking anything
    since no one is using this tag yet.

commit 308df1ed23bc2d979ef8674a0785068ccd4eb555
Author: Matuzalém Teles <matuzalemteles@gmail.com>
Date:   Tue Feb 16 13:43:34 2021 -0300

    LPS-123793 Adds the FormFieldSettings component
    
    This component maintains a FormProvider to handle the structure of the Field's settingsContext, every Field contains the structure of a form that is used within the Sidebar to render a form where it allows editing the properties of the field rendered in the Builder.
    
    FieldSettings needs its own Provider and reducers, it manipulates settingsContext page data and has different behaviors. But he also needs to talk to the root FormProvider and sync some data.
    
    The use of ConfigProvider is still due to the fact that the Sidebar is in Metal and is not rendered on the same structure and cannot have visibility of the application's root Config.
    
    Ideally, we need to have a more elegant way of keeping two FormProvider taking care of two different data but that are dependents.
    
    Some reasons for having the sync is that the Builder root FormProvider reducers have the responsibility to update the information within the settingsContext which could be avoided and done within the FieldSettings reducers to avoid creating any state sync components. This is a research aria of the structural problem that needs to be studied and that could not be done during the migration from Metal.js to React

commit bb9215334d1c3d0ffce5da0313a9b0ba456104b4
Author: Matuzalém Teles <matuzalemteles@gmail.com>
Date:   Tue Feb 16 12:40:24 2021 -0300

    LPS-123793 Fixes error when changing the language back to recently typed text
    
    Sidebar uses the focusedField information to use the settingsContext and render the fields, despite updating the data in the page structure in the builder, the focusedField was not updated and when it caused a rendering in the application it returned to the previous state that was in the focusedField.
    
    The problem is that whenever you update the data in a field, you have to update the data in the focusedField and the page structure to avoid having old data problems, this can be a very silent problem, the focusedField must contain only the field id for reference but not all data, this requires more memory allocation and unnecessary duplication but for that we would have to change a lot of logic, leaving it as a suggestion for the team to change in the future.

    LPS-123794 | LPS-123795 Applies review suggestions
    
    * Use parentField property on useDrop hook as a optional property properly
    * Removes unused code and leftovers of DataConverter migration
    * Removes unnecessary getDropHandler file. Since We're calling Form actions on the new useDrop hook, this file is unnecessary.
    * It also fixes a overTarget error when dragging a field on Table View
    * Instead of DIRECTIONS as a constant for use on resize, use RESIZE_DIRECTIONS
    * Simplifies ResizableColumn responsibilities and props traversing. See: https://github.com/liferay-forms/liferay-portal/pull/132#discussion_r581354059
    * Remove DraggableField component. This component is unnecessary since it just contain a simple markup, doesn't handle logic and can cause a bad idea of handling some Dragging logic.
    * Move FieldDragPreview to a separated file
    * Avoids losing the reference of mousemove and mouseup handlers when rendering using useCallback. See: https://github.com/liferay-forms/liferay-portal/pull/132#discussion_r581323376
    * Move ResizableColumn component to a separated file
    * Removes DRAG_DATA_DEFINITION_FIELD_MOVE const
    * Removes DRAG_FIELDSET_MOVE dragType since We have the same implementation of DRAG_FIELD_TYPE_MOVE on useDrop
    * Fixes drag/drop behaviours on fieldset. It fixes a weird behaviour of dragging a field inside a fieldset. Also, It adds some displayNames for forwardedRef components
    * Avoid surpress eslint for fieldDragPreview effect. When using `ref.current` directly to mutate and remove on unmount there was an error but when storing this ref on a variable inside the effect it will not lose the reference :)
    
    Co-authored-by: Matuzalém Teles <matuzalem.teles@liferay.com>

commit b8051f759d8a489d5e781baeab167490b48a85d0
Author: Diego Nascimento <diego.nascimento@liferay.com>
Date:   Mon Feb 1 16:50:13 2021 -0300

    LPS-123794 Adds custom `useDrag` and `useDrop` and creates more item.types
    
    Created a generic useDrop/useDrag hook for dispatching different actions depending of the type of the Drag.
    
    You may notice We can have two types of Drag:
    
    * You can drag between fields(useDrag from EditorVariant.Column and Pages)
    
    * You can drag between a sidebar and a builder(useDrag defined on Sidebar components)
    
    This knowledge is necessary for deciding which drag action I need to call. The main idea is to call the action ended with `MOVE` when dropping something initialized with values from the drag between fields and `ADD` when dropping something initialized with values from drag from the sidebar.
    
    Also, We can have three different types of a drag item:
    
    * fieldType:
    When the item to be dragged is a field/sidebar field
    
    * dataDefinitionField:
    When the item to be dragged came from App Builder CustomObjectList sidebar
    
    In the future it will be replaced by just `fieldset` or `fieldtype` depending on the context
    
    * fieldset:
    When the item to be dragged came from a fieldset.
    
    Currently, there are 7 types of Drag, combining the already defined types of field/action:
    
    `DragTypes.DRAG_FIELD_TYPE_ADD`
    `DragTypes.DRAG_DATA_DEFINITION_FIELD_MOVE`
    `DragTypes.DRAG_FIELD_TYPE_MOVE`
    `DragTypes.DRAG_FIELDSET_MOVE`
    `DragTypes.DRAG_DATA_DEFINITION_FIELD_ADD`
    `DragTypes.DRAG_FIELD_TYPE_ADD`
    `DragTypes.DRAG_FIELDSET_ADD`

    LPS-123796 Rewrite `withActionableFields` and `withClickableFields` in React
    
    It creates an component called `Actions` where it can be rendered on a field when clicking/hovering on the field and it's controlled by a context called ActionsContext where We could store values of which field is currently being hovered/active and We can reuse this information thru form-builder's React tree
    
    It also creates a new variant called EditorVariant which is responsible for rendering form-builder view exclusive things and removes some responsabilities from the DefaultVariant(user-view) decoupling the builder view from the user view using composition. See more details on: https://docs.google.com/document/d/15i97m9jkNT4Eskuo6P0f0z_aj8isqOmUXvlUXkQxpyY/edit
    
    It also removes `hovered` and `selected` handling from the LayoutProvider, this logic on the LayoutProvider was created for dealing with the old structure(delegating the Actions shell) and it was easily replaced by the ActionsContext

commit 4ad0773ed68c15953fbfd8f6e82192efe2e35bd6
Author: Greg Hurrell <greg.hurrell@liferay.com>
Date:   Wed Nov 25 16:55:34 2020 +0100

    LPS-123550 Turn off JS (and CSS) minifier by default
    
    As per the LPS:
    
    > We will attempt to shift minification from run-time to build-time.
    >
    > Once this is completed and js (and css) resources are minified by
    > default, the next step is to disable run-time minification. Most
    > settings can be found in the #Minifier section of our default
    > portal.properties which includes:
    >
    > ```
    > minifier.enabled
    > minifier.inline.content.cache.enabled
    > minifier.inline.content.cache.skip.css
    > minifier.inline.content.cache.skip.javascript
    > ```
    >
    > The goal of this story is to disable run-time minification of
    > resources by default.
    >
    > # Acceptance Criteria
    >
    > - Minification is no longer enabled by default in production-like setup in portal.properties
    > - A breaking change warning explains this change in behaviour and how to enable it back for those that need it.
    >
    > # Additional Considerations
    >
    > The minifier.enabled property enables both JS and CSS
    > minification. For simplicity, since Simplify CSS minification scheme
    > in DXP is in the short-term roadmap, we should be able to simply
    > disable it now and make sure CSS minification doesn't depend on it
    > before the release of 7.4 GA1

commit 826fad6b355c3318ebafa054bc62bc84c23f01f6
Author: Iván Zaera Avellón <ivan.zaera@liferay.com>
Date:   Fri Nov 20 11:46:42 2020 +0100

    LPS-123157 Add an internal Liferay API to be able to bootstrap federated modules.
    
    This API would only be used from the top level entry points that bootstrap a JS module dependency graph. These are, for example: taglibs, JS portlets, React components inside JSP pages, JS snippets inside JSPs, ... The rest of the gluing is done by the webpack runtime itself but we need to have one method to enter the webpack domain from the Liferay domain.
    
    This is needed because in a regular webpack application, everything is bootstrapped from the HTML file using <script> tags, but we don't have any HTML file in Liferay, as it is based on portlets and its decentralized and modularized. However, we have an equivalent to the regular HTML pages, which is the portal itself, i.e., we model the whole portal as a single webpack application and everything works fine, with the caveat that we need this little code to bootstrap webpack modules (i.e.: adding them to the HTML page using a <script> tag and then initializing and invoking them).
    
    We also have to define two global objects to avoid polluting the `window` namespace with webpack stuff. Those are: `Liferay.Webpack.Container` and `Liferay.Webpack.SharedScope`. The former will contain the objects defined by webpack federated `<script>`s (the default behavior is to put them in `window` but we cannot afford that as there are lots of them). The latter (`SharedScope`) contains the shared module exports (usually returned by modules in `Liferay.Webpack.Container`) and it is only usedby webpack, as opposed to `Container` which is used by webpack and us.
